## Changes Applied

Verification foundation (public worktree, all new unless stated):

S2 paired pin guard:
- `s2-public-pin-red.log` and `s2-private-pin-red.log`: 5/5 assertion failures per repo against the 4.0.1 baseline. Acquisition intentionally not performed (disk gate), so there is no GREEN yet.

S12b/c/d RM3 primitives, review-driven repairs:
- Four primitives now exist as pure Foldkit view builders with no React import. Review found real gaps and they were repaired rather than argued away: the checkbox exposed neither onClick nor onPointerDown, so a live call site that must stop a click from toggling a surrounding summary element could not be ported (now fixed with caller-owned hooks and a propagation test); data-disabled was removed because live never emits it; inputMode/autoComplete were added because live uses them; class assertions were upgraded from two or three tokens to full-constant equality plus frozen literals, so deleting a hover or focus utility now fails.
- The workers corrected their own evidence where a reviewer disproved it, and one claimed doc-vs-code disagreement was withdrawn as a category error.
- Controller closed the last lint findings in the button (0 findings now) and repaired a duplicated block introduced while doing so; final state 19 primitive Scene tests, 15 graph assertions, primitives typecheck 0.

S6a SQLite service (R23/B3):
- New `service.ts` provides explicit async Effect callers plus a compatible synchronous facade over ONE connection owner, and each of the six in-scope contract items has its own behavioural RED: partial-open cleanup preserving the original cause, the exact initial+100ms+200ms retry with a 5s SQLite busy_timeout, same-connection transactions preserving the primary error across rollback AND close failures, no side-effect retry inside a transaction, and serialized ownership through a Semaphore plus Ref rather than a mutable depth counter.
- Deliberate non-use of Effect.tryPromise is recorded with its reason: node:sqlite blocks, so there is no cancellable I/O to abort.
- Not claimed: rich graph hydration, backup/reset/restore draining, per-store PRAGMAs beyond the caller's setup hook, and caller migration (exact call sites reported).
- Controller closed the reported packaging gap: the build now emits a `service` entry, the package exposes `./service` with types, the declarations are emitted by a dedicated tsconfig, and the built subpath imports cleanly. A type error the worker's own typecheck did not catch surfaced on that build and was fixed by restating node:sqlite's unexported bind shape with a SAFETY justification. New suite 11/11, sqlite-memory 140/140, service typecheck 0.

S12b RM3 button primitive and S14a app shell:
- The primitive ports the real RM3 contract, which lives as token-driven class helpers in the runtime-ui app rather than as a React component: the slice corrected that premise instead of inventing a component to port. Scene tests prove click dispatch, disabled refusal, accessible naming and token classes.
- The shell mounts through `Runtime.embed` with a real DOM event path (not a hand-rolled update loop), renders the RM3 primitive plus a foldkit-ui control, and disposes cleanly. Two lifecycle facts were learned and recorded: the container must carry an id for Model preservation, and the runtime replaces the container while mounted and restores it empty on dispose.

S14b wrapper declaration defect (found by the shell slice):
- `@role-model-router/foldkit-ui` emitted 65 declaration files that still imported the upstream bare name `foldkit/...`, because tsc emits types from the vendored sources while only the runtime specifiers were rewritten. At runtime nothing failed; a consumer typecheck did, and the app had shipped a `paths` shim to work around it.
- Fixed in the wrapper build with a declaration post-pass (bare `foldkit` specifiers rewritten to the first-party package), rebuilt to 0 bare occurrences, removed the app's shim, and re-verified the app typecheck (exit 0) and mount test. A maintained assertion now fails if any wrapper declaration names an upstream package again (graph test 15/15).

S3d runtime graph probe (and the defect it found):
- The static graph claim was not enough: a runtime probe that imports every built wrapper entry found 15 of 85 failing, 14 of them genuine. Root cause was in the effect wrapper's entry discovery (`entries.mjs`), which only scanned the Effect, effect-mq and sqlite sources, so subpaths that the browser platform sources import (`Pipeable`, `Runtime`, `Deferred`, `Effectable`, `PlatformError`, `http/HttpClientError`) were never built to `dist`. The probe failure is the reason this was caught.
- Controller fix: `entries.mjs` now also scans `vendor/effect/packages/platform/browser/src`; the wrapper rebuilt and the probe reports 85 total, 84 loaded, with the single remaining non-load being the `foldkit/test/vitest` entry that is only valid inside the vitest runner. Core stayed 200/200 after the rebuild. A DOM environment is still absent, so no browser service is exercised for real yet.

S3d browser runtime lane:
- Under happy-dom 20.14.6 the committed smoke test proves DOM globals and exercises two browser services for real (BrowserKeyValueStore over localStorage, BrowserCrypto including crypto.subtle); IndexedDB, Web Locks, Geolocation, Clipboard and Permissions are skipped with the missing-API reason in the test name rather than passing silently.
- The runtime graph is now a maintained assertion: 85 entries, 84 loads outside the runner, and exactly one permitted non-load named and message-asserted. Static graph-closure stays 14/14. Controller re-ran all three (4 passed/5 skipped, 1/1, 14/14).
- Controller decision recorded: happy-dom is the environment the vendored Foldkit test configs themselves use, and it was added as a devDependency with a verified frozen lockfile. It is a DOM simulation, not a browser, so no rendering, layout, origin/quota or real-browser API claim follows from it.

S3c browser platform wrapper:
- Two REDs: a setup RED for the missing package, then a behavioural RED when the platform package existed but the Foldkit peer still pointed at the unbuilt `@effect/platform-browser` ("the foldkit peer must point at the first-party browser platform wrapper"). GREEN 14/14 across the three wrappers.
- The worker disclosed one out-of-list edit (`foldkit/build.mjs` esbuild onResolve rewrite) without which the peer repoint would be metadata only; the graph test proves the built dist imports the workspace specifier, so the deviation is accepted and recorded.
- Controller lockfile wiring: the peer specifier change made the lockfile stale; the first frozen install refused it, my first manual edit malformed the importer, the frozen check caught that, and the repaired block now passes "Lockfile is up to date" with both links present.

S3b Foldkit wrappers:
- Three REDs captured: a setup RED (missing entries module), a behavioural RED (a missing mapped source was silently skipped), and a genuine graph-level RED produced by injecting `import "node:fs"` into the built bundle, which the test caught as a browser-graph violation before the file was restored. GREEN 10/10; both builds exit 0 with only the expected vendored-tsconfig warning; 67/67 published subpaths covered.

S8a follow-up:
- The reviewer rejected the slice's "lint clean on all four files" claim and was right: a relative lint target resolved against the main checkout from this linked worktree, scanned zero files and reported clean. With absolute paths the new test had three unsafety-commented assertions, which the controller fixed (typed context, instanceof narrowing, SAFETY comment) and re-verified clean. The cancel-watch path is now covered by two tests proving the handler's own signal fires on an operator cancel and that the failure carries the named error; the honest limit (the class name is not observable through the worker) is recorded in the test and evidence.

S3a Foldkit vendoring:
- Pin test written first: 6/6 failed against a missing manifest and missing UPSTREAMS entry, then 6/6 passed after the exact-tag sync. The requested tag `v0.169.0` does not exist; the package-scoped `foldkit@0.169.0` was verified to resolve to the required commit before use, and the version map is asserted exactly because only three of the shipped packages are on 0.169.0.

S7b route producer wiring:
- RED is the bypass proof, not a missing module: the instrumented Layer counted 0 decisions while the caller still used the kernel (`expected +0 to be 1`). GREEN 3/3, full package 21/21, package typecheck 0.

S8a worker cancellation:
- RED reported `context.signal was undefined`, captured against the pre-fix thunk with the file restored byte-identically. GREEN proves the handler observes abort under a short `Effect.timeout`; 13 queue suites / 43 tests and the app typecheck pass. The vendored rule that decides this is `internal/effect.ts:1166` (`withSignal = thunk arity`).

S2 paired Effect pin (both repos):
- Pin guards failed 5/5 on 4.0.1 in each repo (`s2-pin-red-public.log`, `s2-pin-red-private.log`), then passed 5/5 after the exact-tag sync (`s2-pin-green-*.log`). Untouched upstream byte acquisition used the reviewed characterisation exception, not a behavioural RED: vendor `--verify` exit 0 before and after, paired digests identical, prior blocked-attempt evidence preserved.
- The controller independently re-ran `--verify` (exit 0, same digest both repos) and the pin tests (5/5 both repos).

S7 routing Effect producer:
- First failure was the missing module only; the six behavioural assertions (ties, empty pool, hard eligibility, arm/effort identity, effort fallback, byte equality) are the real gate and pass (`s7-red.log`, `s7-green.log`). The module reads the VENDORED 4.0.1 API (Context.Service, Layer.succeed, Effect.sync/provide/runSync) with file and line citations, and does not invent a signature.

S5 bounded health wait:
- RED `/115-effect-foldkit-runtime-migration/evidence/logs/s5-red.log` shows the hanging-peer cases failing as `expected 'hung' to be 'rejected'` against the pre-fix module, GREEN `s5-green.log` 3/3, tsc `s5-tsc.log`, existing 6 tests still green (`s5-existing.log`), stability `s5-stability.log`/`s5-fullsuite-stability.log`, measured bound `s5-bounded-duration.log`.

S9b EventLog recovery (private):
- Three review-driven rounds, each with a failing test first: post-COMMIT masking, then the retention-path rollback, the unreadable-handle path and the misleading unrecoverable wording. Round logs `s9b-red.log`, `s9b-f1f2-red.log`, `s9b-p3-red.log` and their green counterparts in the private run directory; the controller re-ran the suite (9/9) and the idempotency, stream and SP47 suites.

S9a capture drain join (private):
- RED `s9a-red.log` 4/4 failing on the defect (close returned while the pass was inside the before_ack hook, a second pass started after close, a second close threw on released handles, and a wedged pass closed in 13 ms), GREEN `s9a-green.log` 4/4, existing subsets `s9a-existing.log`, and the pre-existing-failure proof `s9a-existing-baseline-check.log` re-verified by the reviewer.

S10 capture-queue close (private):
- Two rounds: close-during-flush no-op and post-close entry points first, then the four review disclosures. Round logs `s10-red-capture-queue-close.log`, `s10-red2-capture-queue-close-review.log` and greens; the controller re-ran 25/25 across the new and existing queue suites.

S15a/S15c diagnostics read (R21 server half):
- RED 13 of 14 against a stub, then the independent review produced five reproducible defects and each was fixed from its own failing input: an 18,176-byte body against a 16 KiB cap, a 400 for disabled plus an invalid cursor, a false stale state from list order, dropped masked by stale, and a past-end cursor reported as ok.

S15b diagnostics page (R21 UI half):
- RED 12 of 12 against the routed shell, then three targeted hazard REDs: disabled rendered as an error, denied collapsed into empty, and stale masking a concurrent dropped condition. The fetch is a Command with result Messages, and the loading test asserts the reader received a real AbortSignal (it only does because the thunk declares a parameter).

S14b routing:
- Three REDs including two mutations: an init that ignored the URL timed out on the diagnostics route, and an update that ignored ChangedUrl left the index view rendered. Routing is runtime-owned; the shell has no listener of its own.

- Controller: refreshed the changed-path enumeration and the requirement dispositions after each slice.

## Plan Deviations

- S2 acquisition (vendor sync, wrapper version, build, consumer re-run) is not performed: the C: volume was below the planned 256 MiB gate at execution time (observed 83 MiB, later 53–87 MiB). Pin tests are committed and RED; the slice remains open. No deletion of unrelated user data was attempted.
- The selfcheck exposes ten controls rather than the eight first drafted, because review showed error/terminal-stream were uncovered. No source requirement fixes the count.
- A geometry-validity guard (`invalid_capture`) was added inside the structural comparator because a NaN rectangle could make an invalid capture compare equal; the adapter still owns the real capture.
- Root `package.json`/lockfile/CI were modified by the controller (single-writer rule), not by a worker, for the parity lane.

Audit Execution Mode: `self-audit` (Phase 3 is not yet audited; the slice reviews recorded above are read-only code reviews of individual slices, not a phase audit).
Delegation Override Reason: the phase is still in draft, so no audit handoff is meaningful until the requirement set is complete.

Phase 3 has NOT been audited. This artifact makes no coverage or approval claim for the phase, and the gate lines below remain FAIL.

- Coverage: FAIL (requirements R9, R10, R12, R13, R15, R16, R17, R18, R19, R20, R21, R24, R27, R28, R31, R32, R33, R37 and the SRC rows born blocked have no implementation).
- Approval: FAIL (no phase audit has run, so nothing can be approved).

- Audit: FAIL (not run).

## Gaps Found

- R4 export-surface diff is an additive check over the vendored sources and package map; it is not a public-API compatibility certification.
- R5 has no real-browser capture: happy-dom is a DOM simulation, so rendering, layout, origin/quota, CSP, worker and bundler behaviour are unproven.
- R7/R8 cover four controls only; select, dialog and the rest are absent, and no pixel/ARIA parity against the React app is measured.
- R11 has no routing yet; the shell mounts one view.
- R21 has no page and no read endpoint yet.
- R23 lists the clauses it does not implement: rich graph hydration, backup/reset/restore owner draining, arbitrary per-store PRAGMAs, and caller migration away from direct node:sqlite use.
- R26 leaves four production sweeps uncancellable, stated at their call sites.
- R29/R30 remain partial in the private repository; the request-path capture flush is still not joined during close.
- Two pre-existing runtime-graph capture tests fail in the private tree (recorded, not caused by this run).
- The vendored payloads are upstream bytes: identity comes from the manifests and digests, not from a first-party review of 3,745 paths.

- R10 | Status: blocked | Rationale: the chart renderer is now a documented pure function of (series, state) with loading, error, empty, stale, truncated and ready as distinct renderable states, caller-supplied ages and drop counts, and truncation visible in both a notice and the table caption (10 Scene tests, each state with its own mutation RED). - R11 | Status: blocked | Rationale: the shell mounts through the real runtime and now routes with URL-derived init and runtime-owned navigation (5/5 tests, typecheck 0), but further route families and the remaining shell work are open. | Blocking Evidence: `/.recursive/run/115-effect-foldkit-runtime-migration/00-requirements.md`
- R12 | Status: blocked | Rationale: the refresh path is correct under races, navigation and gaps for both diagnostics and read routes. One shell-owned injectable subscription; no route-owned source; a gap resyncs with no later event and now REFETCHES read routes rather than showing stale rows; payloads are applied before the page may claim live; failures are sticky and route-attributed; navigation fences pending resyncs; older successes and failures resolving last cannot win; the stale banner is asserted present while stale and absent while live. A request-inventory comparator captures method, path, query, body, headers, multiplicity and status and is proven on one new route and one real two-sided pair that surfaced a genuine header divergence. Remaining: the comparator covers two routes, not the inventory; streams, redirects, retries and TLS are outside it. | Blocking Evidence: `/.recursive/run/115-effect-foldkit-runtime-migration/00-requirements.md`
- R13 | Status: blocked | Rationale: twelve routes are real and the wave-2 pattern is one shared surface. The inventory is complete and checkable (recursive scan of all 75 legacy tsx files, registered-path comparison, verified rows gated on a NAMED test, per-row host endpoints). Wave 1 makes index, not-found and the shell real; wave 2 now has twelve routes (observability plus eleven readbacks) through ONE factory, parser table, generic command and renderer, each with success/empty/loading with aria-busy/error on the DOM, exact per-route error phrasing, distinct 401/403, invalid-200 rejection and a fetch-count refresh test on the single subscription. Client paths are tied to PARSED host registrations rather than a substring search, a successful gap refetch clears stale while a failed one keeps it, and the default shell discovers the operator token from storage. Totals: 62 absent, 1 wired, 12 verified. Remaining: 62 routes unported, wave 4 blocked on four missing host endpoints. | Blocking Evidence: `/.recursive/run/115-effect-foldkit-runtime-migration/00-requirements.md`

## Repair Work Performed

Repairs made during this phase, each after a review or a controller error, with what was actually wrong:

- S1a request comparator: same-step repeated-read pairing and missing read causality were found in review; the comparator gained failing cases for both before the fix.
- S1b target guard: a raw-URL normalization bypass and missing subtree containment were found in review; both got their own failing tests first.
- S8a cancellation: the slice claimed the lint was clean on four files. A relative lint target resolved against the main checkout from this linked worktree, scanned zero files and reported clean. With absolute paths the new test had three unjustified assertions, which were typed or given SAFETY justifications; the test header also misstated the RED, which was corrected to the recorded failure.
- S12b/S12c/S12d primitives: review found the checkbox could not host a real call site that needs to stop a click from toggling a surrounding summary element (fixed with caller-owned hooks), a non-live `data-disabled` attribute (removed), missing `inputMode`/`autoComplete` (added), and class assertions that pinned only two or three tokens (now full-string equality plus frozen literals). One claimed doc-vs-code disagreement was withdrawn as a category error.
- S14a shell: review placed a packaging HOLD because the test lane depended on hand-made junctions. The devDependencies were declared, the junctions deleted, the frozen install re-verified, the vacuous disposal assertion replaced with one on rendered text, and the test file moved inside the typecheck.
- S14b wrapper declarations: the foldkit-ui build emitted 65 declaration files importing the upstream bare name, invisible at runtime and fatal to a consumer typecheck. The build gained a declaration post-pass, the app dropped its paths shim, and a maintained assertion now fails if any wrapper declaration names an upstream package.
- S6a SQLite service: the slice's own typecheck did not cover the built entry. The controller added the package service export and declarations, which surfaced a real type error (node:sqlite binds an unexported SQLInputValue shape); it was fixed with a restated type and a SAFETY justification.
- Controller error during this phase: a full-file write to this artifact truncated it to 317 lines because the read used a line limit smaller than the file. The changed-path enumeration, `## Requirement Completion Status`, `## Repair Work Performed` and `## Audit Verdict` were regenerated; the SRC rows were restated conservatively rather than reconstructed from memory.


## Status Vocabulary and NOT RUN inventory

R18 requires PASS, FAIL, NOT RUN, INCONCLUSIVE and reviewed-N/A to be distinguishable, and requires that private content stays private while public references stay safe. This section is the authoritative vocabulary for the artifact.

- PASS: an assertion ran in this run and succeeded, with a log path cited in the row that claims it.
- FAIL: an assertion ran and failed; a failing result is never rounded up to PASS. Review HOLD verdicts are recorded as HOLD, not as FAIL and not as PASS.
- NOT RUN: no assertion ran. Listed below rather than implied.
- INCONCLUSIVE: something ran but cannot settle the claim (for example a platform-limited skip, or a probe that the environment cannot exercise).
- reviewed-N/A: the clause does not apply to this tree, with the reason recorded (for example a private-only module in a public artifact).

### NOT RUN or INCONCLUSIVE in this run

- Packaged artifact, packaged start, packaged identity and packaged real-client parity: NOT RUN. Packaging refuses this worktree (dirty) and the private distribution records its paired public tree from a commit, so no artifact could be produced without an owner decision.
- SEA tests named by R22: NOT RUN.
- POSIX grace-then-SIGKILL escalation timing: INCONCLUSIVE on this host - the tests are skipped because Windows has no console graceful signal for these Node children.
- Real-browser CORS/preflight, TLS and deployed-asset serving: NOT RUN.
- Viewport layout, focus painting and screen-reader announcements: NOT RUN as behaviour; asserted only as classes and attributes.
- Live provider inference through a packaged router (R15): NOT RUN.
- Release/dev/candidate comparison against v0.0.20 (R16): NOT RUN.
- Cross-surface persistence journeys J1-J6 (R17): NOT RUN.
- The cross-repo legacy-sqlite suites: NOT RUN unless ROLE_MODEL_PUBLIC_WORKTREE is provided; the prerequisite is named rather than the failure hidden.

### Privacy posture

- Private repository evidence is summarised in `evidence/other/private-slices.md`; raw private logs stay in the private worktree.
- The artifact references the private worktree path once, as the diff basis for the paired audit. That is a local path, not private content: no private source, schema, secret or payload is quoted publicly.
- No secret, token or credential value appears in this artifact; the only header names mentioned are the ones the host rejects.
